  Hi, Greg & List,
  I've read some online references to this incident being caused
  by a radio-controlled [RC] toy helicopter or other small flying
  light-emitting diode [LED] equipped hobbyist platform of some
  kind. LED kits for various RC flying aircraft and mini-
  helicopters are becoming quite popular, and have been for the
  last several years that such night-flying LED kits (which can
  also be pro- grammed to emit differing colors, display patterns,
  and also be turned off and on in flight by use of the same RC
  controllers and mods to the ground control rigs used by expert
  hobbyists) have been available in hobby stores and via online
  shops that specialize in night-flying RC/LED rigs and equipment-
  -just go online to confirm this long-standing night itme hobby
  activity.
  There were a series of incidents, initially reported in local
  papers in the central and southern California valley areas as
  possible UFOs, of blue and bluish-white (and other colors and
  flashing/morphing display patterns observed from some distance)
  being observed to be dashing around the sky, hovering, and
  performing other acrobatic motions, including sudden black out
  or disappearance and equally unexpected reappearance in another
  part of the night sky. It turns out, in every case I've read
  about and followed up on, including one incident where a curious
  witness got in their car and drove toward the area where the
  lights were seen, that these were all RC controlled hobbyist
  small helicopter and aircraft models equipped with LED rigs of
  both off the shelf purchased variety and some more sophisticated
  models with custom, programmable-in- flight LED systems on-
  board.
  I'm familiar with this syndrome, of initial misidentification,
  due to the fact that a brother-in-law of mine used to work in
  the hobbyist RC business, at two different shops in the NorCal
  area, and the LED kits available years ago sold quite well, and
  customers would sometimes mention at purchase that one purpose,
  other than being able to fly at night after work, or whenever
  they wanted at night, was to not only be able to be able to
  adequately track their RC craft in the dark so that they could
  be more easily controlled and monitored, but to, at times,
  impress and goof on "the rubes" who might think they were
  observing bizarre light phenomena or, wait for it... UFOs.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/nov/m09-006.shtml[12/12/2011 09:40:24]
Re: Blue UFO Video Centreville Virginia


  There have even been some cases where people have made UFO
  shaped flying wings or circles with aerodynamic cut-outs covered
  with LEDs who have gotten in trouble for flying their craft
  deliberately over nearby public venues and beaches at night to
  create a UFO misidentification incident, which they apparently
  get some kind of sick kick and lulz from doing. One guy and his
  partner even said so directly in newspaper coverage they
  received after they were caught by some local cops, were warned
  not to continue these intentional attempts to "scare the
  natives" for their personal pathological gratifica- tion and
  prankster instincts, and said in a newpaper interview I read
  that they also intended to sell their craft to others who also
  might enjoy freaking people out. All of this coverage is online.
  So, as far as the incident Greg notes above at the link given, I
  suspect that this incident is just another case of such amateur
  pranksters and hobbyists having their fun at public expense.

  Steve

Re: Change In Refractive Index Of Space Near UFO

  Steve,
  I will be happy to respond more thoroughly, but I am unable to
  do so immediately. I have just purchased a home, which is
  taking all my free time.
  However, I will respond here to the extent of asking whether you
  think you read my original message carefully. I did _not_ say I
  have only one "authentic" photo of a UFO. I said that I estimate
  that only one, out of perhaps some 10,000 to 20,000 sent to
  NUFORC, is, in my opinion, likely to be demonstrably of a UFO.
  That implies a percentage of the reports sent to us. The
  overwhelming majority of photos sent to NUFORC, paraded about in
  the news media, or discussed in UFO forums, are not, in my
  opinion, demonstrably of genuine UFO's.
  Also, have you attended any of my public presentations, in which
  I play videos, and show still photos, which I consider to be of
  authentic UFO's?? I usually play three or four of those videos,
  which, when combined with the reports from individuals who
  witnessed the object photographed, lead me to believe that the
  object captured on film was an authentic UFO, i.e. a
  sophisticated craft which appears not to have originated on our
  planet. I have played them many times, but given that I do not
  hold the copyright for them, I have hesitated to post them to
  the NUFORC website, save for the video from Sandia National
  Laboratories, captured on August 15, 1999. I believe that file
  has to be refreshed. Please see the reports on the NUFORC site
  for the evening of that date. It was very dramatic...and it was
  followed by a similar event some 21 minutes later. I have had
  witnesses to both events on both Coast to Coast, and on the Jeff
  Rense radio program.
  I hope that helps you understand my view of photos of UFOs.

  PD
  NUFORC

                                   Re: Change In Refractive Index Of Space Near UFO
  From: William Treurniet <wtreurniet.nul>
  Date: Wed, 10 Nov 2010 10:59:24 -0500
  Archived: Wed, 10 Nov 2010 11:58:16 -0400
  Subject: Re: Change In Refractive Index Of Space Near UFO

  >From: Bruce Maccabee<brumac.nul>
  >To: post.nul
  >Date: Mon, 8 Nov 2010 14:04:55 -0500 (EST)
  >Subject: Re: Change In Refractive Index Of Space Near UFO
  >>From: Ray Dickenson<r.dickenson.nul>
  >>To:<post.nul>
  >>Date: Mon, 8 Nov 2010 17:13:24 -0000
  >>Subject: Re: Change In Refractive Index Of Space Near UFO
  >>>From: William Treurniet<wtreurniet.nul>
  >>>Date: Sat, 06 Nov 2010 14:25:56 -0400
  >>>Archived: Sun, 07 Nov 2010 08:10:08 -0400
  >>>Subject: Change In Refractive Index Of Space Near UFO
  ><snip>
  >>>My analysis showed that the object was accompanied by a toroidal
  >>>optical effect, and where this torus crossed the rainbow, the
  >>>colors were shifted spatially. This suggested that the space
  >>>occupied by the torus had an altered index of refraction.
  ><snip>
  >>That's logical. An inertial-field, or even a high static field,
  >>will change electron energies within it, thereby changing the
  >>freqs - colors - of photons passing through that atmosphere or
  >>emitted by it.
  >Anyone contemplating fields around UFOs should consider the following
  >case of a field remaining after a UFO apparently had departed:
  >http://www.brumac.8k.com/MagneticUFO/MagneticUFO.html
  In this very interesting case, a pulsating, magnetic field
  gradient with no apparent cause was able to linger for a day or
  more. It's almost as if the property of space were changed, and
  then slowly returned to normal. This would not be expected if
  the UFO propulsion system was based on a high-voltage static
  electric field, such as is needed to get the Biefeld-Brown
  effect.
  It is unlikely that a contemporary physics theory could explain
  what was observed. Maybe less accepted theories based on the
  idea of an aether would be useful here. A magnetic field as a
  particular state of the aether might be able to persist after
  the magnetizing influence has gone.
  For example, Stoyan Sarg's Basic Structure of Matter (BSM)
  theory holds that space is filled with a lattice structure held
  together by a primeval force he calls supergravitation. Each
  node of the lattice has certain dynamic properties that account
  for things like light propagation and electric and magnetic
  fields. Perhaps the UFO made this lattice vibrate for a period
  of time after it left, and this ringing is the basis for the
  observed pulsating magnetic field.

  William

  out you leaped at the bait called MJ-12 without checking the
  provenance of the so-called documents. Therefore, we could say
  that you have a tendency in your research of creating a gap.
  Just me, as a sideliner, observing people claiming to be
  players.

Re: Change In Refractive Index Of Space Near UFO

  >>theory holds that space is filled with a lattice structure held
  >>together by a primeval force he calls supergravitation. Each
  >>node of the lattice has certain dynamic properties that account
  >>for things like light propagation and electric and magnetic
  >>fields. Perhaps the UFO made this lattice vibrate for a period
  >>of time after it left, and this ringing is the basis for the
  >>observed pulsating magnetic field.
  >Does Sargoytchev posit his lattice as having some kind of
  >absolute positional property independently of matter and energy?
  >If so, then this explanation would not work, surely, since the
  >movement of matter relative to the lattice would mean that the
  >persistence of the effect would not be localised to the
  >arrangement of matter occupying the area of the lattice where
  >the effect was first produced?
  >We would experience the effect/disturbance as something shifting
  >through space (Unless, of course, our planet is uniquely and
  >permanently aligned to the lattice, in which case somebody needs
  >to inform the Pope that there was no need for all the
  >backtracking that has gone on since Galileo was ticked off for
  >suggesting otherwise - not an entirely facetious point since
  >Joseph Ratzinger was formerly head of The Congregation for the
  >Doctrine of the Faith, the successor organisation to The Roman
  >Inquisition to which Galileo was denounced for his
  >Copernicanism. As Tyco Brahe might have observed, 'what goes
  >around, comes around'!).
  Ha, you are right, Gerald, I had not thought it through. As I
  understand it, the lattice in the theory is an absolute frame of
  reference. Each existing galaxy grew its own lattice structure
  as it formed, and interfaced with adjacent galaxies. So the part
  of the lattice disturbed by the UFO would immediately be left
  behind as the earth moved on in its orbit.
  A way to salvage this explanation is to propose that the
  disturbance moved like a wave through the stationary lattice in
  order to maintain its position relative to that particular
  location on earth. In the theory, the lattice is less dense than
  the structures that make up sub-atomic matter. Resistance to the
  acceleration of matter through the lattice accounts for inertia.
  The theory also says that a permanent magnetic line forms a
  closed loop in the lattice.
  If the pulsating disturbance in the lattice made it more
  difficult for matter to move through it, it would have been
  anchored to the local matter such as the trees. Either the trees
  or the disturbance in the lattice would have had to move, and
  obviously the trees did not. If the disturbance in the lattice
  gradually lost energy to allow the trees to overcome the
  increased inertia, or to propagate through the lattice as the
  earth moved, the disturbance would have dissipated over time as
  was observed.
  There are uncertainties with this explanation that would need to
  be worked out, but you get the general idea.

  William

Re: William Cooper Revisited

  Anyone who has the 1968 Congressional Hearings text on their
  shelves can find the following biography on p.212, or go to
  http://files.ncas.org/ufosymposium/friedman.html
  Stanton T. Friedman
  Born -- July 29,1934, Elizabeth, New Jersey.
  B.Sc. -- Physics, M.Sc. -- Physics, University of Chicago 1955,
  1956.
  Since 1966 -- Westinghouse Astronuclear Laboratory, Pittsburgh;
  NERVA nuclear rocket Program -- Fellow Scientist concerned
  primarily with radiation shielding experiments and nuclear
  instrumentation.
  1963-1966 -- Allison Division, General Motors, Indianapolis,
  Indiana. Military Compact Reactor program (responsible for all
  shielding aspects), magnetohydrodynamics, desalination, other
  projects.
  1959-1963 -- Aerojet General Nucleonics, near San Francisco.
  Development of various nuclear systems for space and terrestrial
  applications; Fusion propulsion for space, consultant on
  radiation shielding.
  1956-1959 -- General Electric, Aircraft Nuclear Propulsion
  Department, Cincinnati. Experimental and analytical aspects of
  radiation shielding for nuclear aircraft.
  Mr. Friedman has a relatively unique background in advanced
  technology, having been actively involved in the development of
  all of the following advanced systems: nuclear aircraft, nuclear
  power for space, terrestrial nuclear power, nuclear rockets,
  fusion rockets.
  Professional affiliations include the American Physical Society,
  the American Nuclear Society, the American Institute of
  Aeronautics and Astronautics, the Aerial Phenomenon Research
  Organization, the National Investigations Committee on Aerial
  Phenomena. Mr. Friedman is on the Board of Directors of the UFO
  Research Institute of Pittsburgh and on the Standards and
  Program Committees of the Shielding Division of the American
  Nuclear Society.
  Mr. Friedman has presented papers at technical society meetings
  and has chaired sessions at such meetings. He has written
  numerous classified and unclassified reports and has published
  articles on UFOs as well as on radiation shielding.
  Mr. Friedman has made dozens of radio and TV appearances across
  the United States and in Canada. These include the Joe Pyne Show
  (Los Angeles - radio), Long John Nebel (New York City), the J.
  P. McCarthy Show in Detroit, all four TV stations in Pittsburgh,
  and others in Raleigh, Akron, Detroit, Baltimore, Toronto, Waco,
  Phoenix, Calgary, Albuquerque, etc.
  Mr. Friedman, his wife, and three children reside at 702
  Summerlea Street in Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania.
  ----
  Martin Shough

  From: Martin Shough <parcellular.nul>
  Date: Thu, 11 Nov 2010 11:42:55 -0000
  Archived: Thu, 11 Nov 2010 07:10:13 -0400
  Subject: Re: Change In Refractive Index Of Space Near UFO

  >From: Gerald O'Connell <goc.nul>
  >Date: Wed, 10 Nov 2010 20:31:00 +0000
  >To: post.nul
  >Subject: Re: Change In Refractive Index Of Space Near UFO
  >>From: William Treurniet <wtreurniet.nul>
  >>To: post.nul
  >>Date: Wed, 10 Nov 2010 10:59:24 -0500
  >>Subject: Re: Change In Refractive Index Of Space Near UFO
  <snip>
  >>For example, Stoyan Sarg's Basic Structure of Matter (BSM)
  >>theory holds that space is filled with a lattice structure held
  >>together by a primeval force he calls supergravitation. Each
  >>node of the lattice has certain dynamic properties that account
  >>for things like light propagation and electric and magnetic
  >>fields. Perhaps the UFO made this lattice vibrate for a period
  >>of time after it left, and this ringing is the basis for the
  >>observed pulsating magnetic field.
  >Does Sargoytchev posit his lattice as having some kind of
  >absolute positional property independently of matter and
  >energy?
  >If so, then this explanation would not work, surely, since the
  >movement of matter relative to the lattice would mean that the
  >persistence of the effect would not be localised to the
  >arrangement of matter occupying the area of the lattice where
  >the effect was first produced?
  >We would experience the effect/disturbance as something
  >shifting
  >through space
  I think Gerald is correct, if the answer to his question is
  "yes, a space lattice like this would be equivalent to Newtonian
  absolute space or the local background spacetime of string
  theories". To make a workable theory of this type requires that
  the theory of the particle trajectories is truly background-
  independent.
  In my opinion it is helpful to put this a different way: the
  particle trajectories themselves are to contain or embody all of
  the information that would be required of the background space.
  I think this demands that the lattice be a nonlocal lattice, and
  that "particles" be nonlocally identified (in an underlying
  theory) both with their trajectories and with the lattice.
  As a hostage to fortune I'll predict that this will turn out to
  mean that the discreteness of space(time) is only locally and
  emergently a matter of volume, area or length, quantisation
  being more fundamentally a matter of _direction_ - thus radial
  angle, linking QG to the phase space of QM and spin. Of course I
  only partially understand what I mean by that ;-)

  Martin Shough

  proponents.

  less-than-explosive charge that Stan wasn't _currently_ working
  as a nuclear physicist.

Re: Change In Refractive Index Of Space Near UFO

  >only partially understand what I mean by that ;-)
  Thanks for that elegant clarification Martin.
  Our informal conceptual framework for discussing such radical
  fundamentals isn't very helpful is it? My problem is: I can't
  conceive of 'direction' without assuming a pre-existing
  'framework' (for want of a better term) that involves volume,
  area and length. So it feels like a bit of a 'cheat' to call on
  the concept of direction to explain an idea of quantisation that
  gives rise to local space/time as we experience it. In my mind,
  at least, the same problem occurs with the terms 'radial angle'
  and 'spin'.
  I'm not complaining. Ignoring the obvious explanation (which is
  that I just don't 'get it'), I think the problem is inevitable.
  There must come a point in laying bare these radical
  fundamentals where our terminology breaks down and we find
  ourselves having to pull ourselves up by our own bootstraps.
  Maybe that's what the universe is having to do all the time...
  --
  Gerald O'Connell

  >And this, from the piece in question, baffles me:
  >"Astronomers' best guess is that the bubbles were created by an
  >eruption from a supersized black hole at the centre of our
  >galaxy."
  >So how does gas escape from a black hole, especially a
  >'supersized' (would that count as a 'Big Black'?) one?
  Ha! Right Gerald,
  You've just picked-up on a science cover-up. The initial
  'sexy/scary' claim of black holes defined them as having
  infinite mass/density and so infinite 'gravity' and so an 'event
  horizon' that nothing could escape and so they were
  'singularities' in space/time.
  So far, none of those attributes have been observed in the
  visible universe but the mainstream scientists are loth to lose
  a 'sexy/scary' fund-raiser. So they've been busy 'redefining'
  putative black holes until now they claim their existence from
  almost _any_ indications of high mass/density, like galactic
  cores, neutron stars, quasars etc.
  However there's an obstreporous bunch of younger physicists
  who've dared to say "Black Holes don't exist" - and, for several
  reasons (of basic physics), I fully agree with them.

  Cheers
  Ray D
  BTW - long time ago, after reading that 'Brief History' - I
  wrote to Hawking asking a couple of pointed questions about his
  use of the word 'infinite' (and therefore of the 'singularity'
  concept), but his secretary wrote back claiming he was too ill
  to answer. He never did.

  Date: Fri, 12 Nov 2010 01:41:45 -0700

  Subject: Re: Giant Space Bubbles Baffle Scientists

  >>find out the udder truth:
  >>http://tinyurl.com/2ehxre3
  >And this, from the piece in question, baffles me:
  >"Astronomers' best guess is that the bubbles were created by an
  >eruption from a supersized black hole at the centre of our
  >galaxy."
  >So how does gas escape from a black hole, especially a
  >'supersized' (would that count as a 'Big Black'?) one?
  >Probably the terminology is just loose, but still, these kind of
  >statements are perplexing.

  Hi Gerald,
  While not an expert on black hole dynamics, I can say that
  infalling material is not actually 'lost' to outside observers
  until it crosses the event horizon radius, which can be large
  indeed (some millions of kilometers in the case of the Milky Way
  central black hole Sgr A*). There can be quite a ruckus visible
  *outside* the horizon, with colliding infalling material
  generating tremendous heat and pressure. The result can be an
  'eruption' of outward flowing matter and radiation, generally
  focused into jets aligned with the hole's magnetic field.
  Assuming the image in the article reflects the actual data, I am
  struck by the precise alignment perpendicular to the galactic
  plane, although I can see how the angular momentum of a central
  black hole would be dominated by that captured from infalling
  material, and hence (over long timescales) acquire the general
  orientation of the galactic angular momentum.
  The alignment was fortunate in any case, as I imagine had we
  been in the path of one of these gamma-ray 'lobes', it would
  have been a rough ride indeed. The article indicates that these
  features are "thought to be millions of years old", which gives
  me the impression (via the Principle of Least Astonishment and
  the age of the galaxy) that such outbursts must be fairly common
  in the Milky Way's history, whether or not they derive from
  black hole accretion.

  Mike

                                            Re: Giant Space Bubbles Baffle Scientists
  From: Kathy Kasten <catraja.nul>
  Date: Thu, 11 Nov 2010 21:53:46 +0000
  Archived: Fri, 12 Nov 2010 06:13:26 -0500
  Subject: Re: Giant Space Bubbles Baffle Scientists

  >From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>
  >To: post.nul
  >Date: Thu, 11 Nov 2010 09:36:25 -0500 (EST)
  >Subject: Giant Space Bubbles Baffle Scientists
  >Just when you thought we knew everything about the Milky Way we
  >find out the udder truth:
  >http://tinyurl.com/2ehxre3

  At last Tuesday's meeting of the Sonora Astronomical Society,
  our guest speaker was Greg Doppmann of NOAO in Tucson.
  (NOAO=National Optical Astronomy Observatory) The title of his
  talk was "Decoding Starlight with Infrared Spectroscopy."
  After providing an explanation of how infrared spectroscopy
  works, including details about absorption lines, etc. During the
  Q&A, an audience member asked Doppmann how exciting a particle
  to the next level, and then letting it slow back down its
  original path/order with the only the emitted photon as the
  evidence of the change was measured. A long ten minute, very
  convoluted explanation was attempted. At the end of Doppmann's
  attempt, I muttered "very tricky," and Doppmann stated "very
  esoteric." So, whether there are planets in the Goldilocks
  region of the cosmos is depended on absorption lines in a
  spectrographic photo of the area. Doppmann's bottom line was
  that we assume that physical laws are the same throughout the
  universe. My comment was, "how do we know that's true until was
  get to another part of the universe."
  Oh, and the new term for cosmic vapor is gaseous water. I think
  that is very funny.
  Last night, on CtoC, I was listening to someone named Barr
  positing that the physical laws we know may be true only for
  this solar system. His opinion was based on the fact that the
  solar system is comprised of planets specific to this solar
  system that act on each other. Other systems in other parts of
  the galaxy have systems different from ours. We might get beyond
  the boundaries of our solar system and find that universe does
  not operate the same everywhere.
  We live in interesting times that call for thinking outside of
  the box created by nineteenth century understanding of physics.

  KK

  http://tinyurl.com/33h6no9

  suspects the central black hole, as massive as 4 million suns,
  is the energy source. Supermassive black holes at the centers of
  other galaxies are known to drive winds and jets so powerful
  that they expel heavy elements and gas from the galaxies. The
  new observations “suggest that our own Milky Way may [also] be
  driving material outwards,” he said."
  http://tinyurl.com/2ehg4vp
  "Astronomers have observed powerful jets blasting from the
  supermassive black holes in other galaxies. These jets are
  fueled by matter falling into the black hole."These monsters,
  once fed, can produce very powerful explosions," said David
  Spergel, chair of Princeton University's astrophysics
  department."

  After my report on first prelude show for BBC special about
  Rendlesham - broadcast on November 1st on BBC Suffolk inside
  Mark Murphy Breakfast show - I was curious to hear will there be
  more announcements in the upcoming shows. It seems that Host
  Mark Murphy is trying hard to keep the anticipation for the
  special. In fact they have even created two jingles for radio
  special so far. As I started to listen BBC Suffolk more and
  more, I could hear that jingles are running during the whole day
  in other shows too. So it seems they are actually trying to make
  this as a radio event. So to remind everyone once more - radio
  host Mark Murphy has stated that he is preparing special show on
  Rendlesham that will be broadcast from Bentwaters Cold War
  Museum on December 17th. It is a joined project between BBC
  Suffolk and newspaper East Anglian Daily Times with their goal
  to "resolve the mystery" as they say.
  I have processed first 18 hours of radio shows. Every breakfast
  show is running for 180 minutes and it airs from Monday to
  Saturday each week.
  I have extracted only Rendlesham references for Week #1 of
  prelude shows (broadcast from November 1 to 6) - shows without
  Rendlesham references are ignored. The list of download link is
  available on the beginning of this message.
  I will continue to monitor the situation and report accordingly.

                                            Re: Giant Space Bubbles Baffle Scientists
  From: Martin Shough <parcellular.nul>
  Date: Fri, 12 Nov 2010 15:47:42 -0000
  Archived: Fri, 12 Nov 2010 11:34:30 -0500
  Subject: Re: Giant Space Bubbles Baffle Scientists

  >From: Ray Dickenson <r.dickenson.nul>
  >To: <post.nul>
  >Date: Thu, 11 Nov 2010 17:52:15 -0000
  >Subject: Re: Giant Space Bubbles Baffle Scientists
  >>From: Gerald O'Connell <goc.nul>
  >>To: post.nul
  >>Date: Thu, 11 Nov 2010 16:38:52 +0000
  >>Subject: Re: Giant Space Bubbles Baffle Scientists
  >>>From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>
  >>>To: post.nul
  >>>Date: Thu, 11 Nov 2010 09:36:25 -0500 (EST)
  >>>Subject: Giant Space Bubbles Baffle Scientists
  >>>http://tinyurl.com/2ehxre3
  >>And this, from the piece in question, baffles me:
  >>"Astronomers' best guess is that the bubbles were created by an
  >>eruption from a supersized black hole at the centre of our
  >>galaxy."
  >>So how does gas escape from a black hole, especially a
  >>'supersized' (would that count as a 'Big Black'?) one?
  >Ha! Right Gerald,
  >You've just picked-up on a science cover-up. The initial
  >'sexy/scary' claim of black holes defined them as having
  >infinite mass/density and so infinite 'gravity' and so an
  >'event horizon' that nothing could escape and so they were
  >'singularities' in space/time.
  The true gravitational singularity (if it occurs) is not at the
  event horizon, Ray, but inside it. All of the well-observed
  violent cosmic activity which has been attributed to black holes
  happens outside the event horizon, due to huge energies where
  infalling material approaches the horizon radius.
  If you've read those "sexy/scary claims" about blackholes made
  by Hawking (as you said you did) and by Penrose (whom you so
  often like to quote) then you must at least have heard of the
  Penrose-Hawking singularity theorems? According to these,
  singularities are inevitable _inside_ blackhole horizonss (not
  outside). And you will also know about the Penrose "cosmic
  censorship" hypothesis, according to which there _cannot_ be
  naked singularities (i.e., outside event horizons). So who
  exactly is covering what, and why?

  Martin Shough

Re: Change In Refractive Index Of Space Near UFO


                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Nov > Nov 12

                                   Re: Change In Refractive Index Of Space Near UFO
  From: Martin Shough <parcellular.nul>
  Date: Fri, 12 Nov 2010 15:48:59 -0000
  Archived: Fri, 12 Nov 2010 11:36:34 -0500
  Subject: Re: Change In Refractive Index Of Space Near UFO

  >From: Gerald O'Connell <goc.nul>
  >Date: Thu, 11 Nov 2010 17:28:46 +0000
  >To: post.nul
  >Subject: Re: Change In Refractive Index Of Space Near UFO
  >>From: Martin Shough <parcellular.nul>
  >>To: <post.nul>
  >>Date: Thu, 11 Nov 2010 11:42:55 -0000
  >>Subject: Re: Change In Refractive Index Of Space Near UFO
  <snip>
  >>As a hostage to fortune I'll predict that this will turn out to
  >>mean that the discreteness of space(time) is only locally and
  >>emergently a matter of volume, area or length, quantisation
  >>being more fundamentally a matter of _direction_ - thus radial
  >>angle, linking QG to the phase space of QM and spin. Of course
  >>I only partially understand what I mean by that ;-)
  >Thanks for that elegant clarification Martin.
  >Our informal conceptual framework for discussing such radical
  >fundamentals isn't very helpful is it? My problem is: I can't
  >conceive of 'direction' without assuming a pre-existing
  >'framework' (for want of a better term) that involves volume,
  >area and length. So it feels like a bit of a 'cheat' to call on
  >the concept of direction to explain an idea of quantisation that
  >gives rise to local space/time as we experience it. In my mind,
  >at least, the same problem occurs with the terms 'radial angle'
  >and 'spin'.
  Ah, but the point is, Gerald, that a discretist theory of this
  type turns the interpretive order upside down. One is not to
  understand quantised spin in terms of direction in local space,
  rather one is to envisage the indefinite number of values of
  radial direction in continuous 4-space as being emergent from
  superposition of an indefinite _number_ of instantiations of a
  more primitive quantum "direction" variable that has only two
  values.
  This would be like a type of statistical mechanics, the
  averaging over of some vary large number of interacting objects.
  Recovered in some approximation to ideal abstraction from the
  system in which it is embedded, each nonlocal binary object (a
  scale-free, mass-free supersymmetric object, neither yet fermion
  nor boson) can be made to reveal this primitive spin up/spin
  down character. Quantised spin can be seen as like a confined
  standing wave which superposes positive-going and negative-going
  cancelled amplitudes to give a flat string, and this string is
  both the relativistically-null spin-one (boson) signal line
  between two spin-half (fermion) electrons and the completed
  "handshake" of a TIQM-type theory of quantum electrodynamics
  (c.f. John Cramer).
  In isolation this unit object can be thought of as "unit scale"
  (having as yet _no_ specified real scale) in a pre-metrical
  theory space, or the _basis state_ of emergent real scale. The
  smallest ensemble representation of this quantum unit (or call
  it a "binit") produces rudimentary rotations of phase through


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/nov/m12-011.shtml[12/12/2011 09:40:45]
Re: Change In Refractive Index Of Space Near UFO

  360 deg on a 2-space slice (which relates the idea to
  holographic theories of cosmology which represent the cosmos on
  a 2-surface from which 3- and 4-volumes are projections), but
  the complete phase space of all N such unit objects is N-
  dimensional, superposing N spin spaces. So the underlying
  quantum structure of the real space would reify the infinite-
  dimensional mathematical structure of the Hilbert space, but
  with an additional displacement into sqrtN separate graph
  origins, making a many-centred network of sqrtN interpermeating
  Hilbert sub-spaces separated by emergent real scale.
  The unit-scale basis state of real scale survives in QED in the
  primitive Pauli exclusion to which electron separation reduces
  in the atomic limit. Outside the atom there is an indefinite
  spectrum of much larger local real scales, each of which
  contains a ghost of its nonlocal basis state _inflated_ over any
  arbitrary interval and preserved as quantum entanglement.
  The emergence of real scale would occur when the whole ensemble
  (exhaustively interconnected as a complete graph) goes over into
  a classical system, in which all unit objects rotate through
  "arbitrary" real 4-angle instead of quantised 2-angle. In this
  process the supersymmetry of the unit objects is broken into
  boson and fermion representations, typified in QED by the photon
  null signal line between two electrons.
  Of course, it is only an idealisation to say that this spacetime
  is smoothly covered by an indefinite number of possible
  trajectories. In fact real photon trajectories are restricted by
  our sqrtN terminal electron positions, an indefinite but not
  necessarily infinite number. So (for QED) emergent radial
  direction for any electron is the set of all possible photon
  directions defined by the network of N other electrons on its
  "celestial sphere".
  What does this imply? Well, it implies that the distribution of
  real directions on this "sky" is for no one particle perfectly
  isotropic, and the distributions are for no two particles
  identical. So - what about mass? Mass must clearly be emergent
  too, donated in a Machian sense by the ensemble. I suggest that
  the gravitational and inertial mass arises as a constraint due
  to the nonlocal ghosts of unit-scale preserved on all these
  radial directions, which behave like ideally-rigid
  incompressible objects all exerting (reciprocally) unit
  compression force (the same unit-incompressibility recoverable
  in the limit as Pauli exclusion). A differential constraint
  occurs because the distribution of radial directions is not
  isotropic, and the map of all these differences becomes the map
  of cosmic mass action. Think of this as resembling a nonlocal
  version of the old LeSage theory combined with a nonlocal
  version of a Higgs-type theory - inertial and gravitational mass
  being donated not by a local system of impeding particles in
  space extraneous to the fermions, but by a constrain intrinsic
  to the underlying supersymmetric partnerships among those
  fermions themselves.
  This all means that there is an indefinite number of possible
  directions which are _not_ realised at a given instant - these
  are _virtual_ directions which could exist, and whose non-
  existence as it were encodes collectively the actual pattern of
  directions which does exist. Differing mathematical ways of
  representing this virtual smoothness allow us to model, on the
  one hand, the continuous sheet of cosmic spacetime and, on the
  other, the virtual particle-filled quantum vacuum. A successful
  network theory would (I like to think) be able to produce both
  of these types of mathematical models as limits of an underlying
  discretist model.
  I think I'd better leave it at that before my brain explodes. I
  hope it helps to explain my thinking, however strange it may
  seem ;-)

  Martin Shough

  Hi Ray
  You may be right. There may be no true black holes. I'm aware
  that there have been and are theorists who think so (if not for
  the same reasons as you). But as _I_ said, where is this
  "cover-up" by scientists that you refer to? Who is doing it? And
  why?
  >Here's the Google results (my arguments are set out more fully
  >in result #12, in my browser)
  >http://tinyurl.com/3xjg4md
  That's a blank search box, but putting in your phrase "Black
  Holes don't exist" evidently produces the results you refer to.
  What I immediately notice from glancing at the first few pages
  is that most of these hits are on work published by George
  Chapline of Lawrence Livermore, and Lawrence Krauss et al. (Case
  Western Reserve), or articles that refer back to their
  publications. These are hardly "young Turks" or estabishment
  outsiders! Chapline was born in 1942, Krauss in 1954. They are
  distinguished senior academics with decades of professional
  respect behind them, and probably not very obstreprous.
  No doubt there are younger colleagues pursuing related ideas.
  But why did you pounce with glee on Gerald's (as it happens,
  inapposite) question to say it was evidence of a "cover-up"?
  Chapline's work was "was performed (in part) under the auspices
  of the U.S. Department of Energy by University of California,
  Lawrence Livermore National Laboratory under contract
  No. W-7405-Eng-48" according to his original paper presented at
  a major Texas conference on relativistic astrophysics. He was
  developing ideas published by Robert Laughlin, a distinguished
  Nobel Prize winner born in 1950. Chapline's and Krauss's
  publications widely reported (as your googling demonstrates) and
  vigorously and openly debated by other physicists, the majority
  of whom tended to disagree with it. It still is, and mostly they
  still do.

  science"?

  Hi Martin
  1) I, and later a number of physicists, have asserted that basic
  physics facts prevent the formation (or even existence) of
  "black holes" in our universe. If you want my own arguments
  they're at www.perceptions.couk.com/uef/nblckhls.html#if -
  that's as simple as I can make it.
  2) The scientific method is theorize - predict - check. If the
  answer is _yes_ you may continue to hold that theory. If the
  answer is _no_ the theory is junked. The answer to checks for
  "black holes" is _no_. But mainstream science, as many times
  before (and today also in the case of the cosmological `standard
  model'), has abondoned the scientific method in order to
  preserve an untrue theory for funding and maybe socio-political
  reasons (loss of face).
  That's the cover-up, and, as said, it's been played out many
  times before, in almost every generation.
  "The Old Theories only die out when the Old Professors die out"
                                                   -- Max Planck

  Cheers
  Ray D

  >I'm afraid not. Sorry.
  I was hoping there was an explanation hidden there somewhere.
  Earlier, I proposed a scenario using an aether theory that might
  explain why the distortion in the lattice (i.e., the pulsating
  magnetic field) would linger in one place relative to the earth.
  I suggested that the distortion was dragged along as the earth
  moved because it was resistant to matter. It took less energy to
  pass the states of the nodes of the lattice to adjacent nodes
  than to break the shape of the distortion. In other words, the
  states of the nodes of the lattice were passed to adjacent nodes
  in order for the distortion to keep its shape.
  Conversely, matter trying to pass through the distortion would
  have shown an increased inertia. The investigators did not
  report any sensations resulting from altered inertia while
  walking through the lattice distortion, but they would not have
  been expecting it either. It might have been interpreted as
  variations in air pressure.

                                                               Re: Black Holes

  >>latest research into the psychology of 'deniers',
  >>'debunkers' and text-book authoritarians - it's at:
  >>www.perceptions.couk.com/warn.txt
  >What research? It's just snark.

  FYI - This from NASA's Chandra X-ray Observatory                          posted
  at NASA's news today.
  Don Ledger
  -----
  Marshall Space Flight Centre
  http://tinyurl.com/328jrzx
  11.15.10
  RELEASE: 10-299
  NASA'S Chandra Finds Youngest Nearby Black Hole
  WASHINGTON -- Astronomers using NASA's Chandra X-ray Observatory
  have found evidence of the youngest black hole known to exist in
  our cosmic neighborhood. The 30-year-old black hole provides a
  unique opportunity to watch this type of object develop from
  infancy.
  The black hole could help scientists better understand how
  massive stars explode, which ones leave behind black holes or
  neutron stars, and the number of black holes in our galaxy and
  others.
  The 30-year-old object is a remnant of SN 1979C, a supernova in
  the galaxy M100 approximately 50 million light years from Earth.
  Data from Chandra, NASA's Swift satellite, the European Space
  Agency's XMM-Newton and the German ROSAT observatory revealed a
  bright source of X-rays that has remained steady during
  observation from 1995 to 2007. This suggests the object is a
  black hole being fed either by material falling into it from the
  supernova or a binary companion.
  "If our interpretation is correct, this is the nearest example
  where the birth of a black hole has been observed," said Daniel
  Patnaude of the Harvard-Smithsonian Center for Astrophysics in


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/nov/m16-005.shtml[12/12/2011 09:40:59]
Re: Black Holes

  Cambridge, Mass. who led the study.
  The scientists think SN 1979C, first discovered by an amateur
  astronomer in 1979, formed when a star about 20 times more
  massive than the sun collapsed. Many new black holes in the
  distant universe previously have been detected in the form of
  gamma-ray bursts (GRBs).
  However, SN 1979C is different because it is much closer and
  belongs to a class of supernovas unlikely to be associated with
  a GRB. Theory predicts most black holes in the universe should
  form when the core of a star collapses and a GRB is not
  produced.
  "This may be the first time the common way of making a black
  hole has been observed," said co-author Abraham Loeb, also of
  the Harvard-Smithsonian Center for Astrophysics. "However, it is
  very difficult to detect this type of black hole birth because
  decades of X-ray observations are needed to make the case."
  The idea of a black hole with an observed age of only about 30
  years is consistent with recent theoretical work. In 2005, a
  theory was presented that the bright optical light of this
  supernova was powered by a jet from a black hole that was unable
  to penetrate the hydrogen envelope of the star to form a GRB.
  The results seen in the observations of SN 1979C fit this theory
  very well.
  Although the evidence points to a newly formed black hole in SN
  1979C, another intriguing possibility is that a young, rapidly
  spinning neutron star with a powerful wind of high energy
  particles could be responsible for the X-ray emission. This
  would make the object in SN 1979C the youngest and brightest
  example of such a "pulsar wind nebula" and the youngest known
  neutron star. The Crab pulsar, the best-known example of a
  bright pulsar wind nebula, is about 950 years old.
  "It's very rewarding to see how the commitment of some of the
  most advanced telescopes in space, like Chandra, can help
  complete the story," said Jon Morse, head of the Astrophysics
  Division at NASA's Science Mission Directorate.
  The results will appear in the New Astronomy journal in a paper
  by Patnaude, Loeb, and Christine Jones of the Harvard-
  Smithsonian Center for Astrophysics.
  NASA's Marshall Space Flight Center in Huntsville, Ala., manages
  the Chandra program for the agency's Science Mission Directorate
  in Washington. The Smithsonian Astrophysical Observatory
  controls Chandra's science and flight operations from Cambridge.
  For more information about Chandra, including images and other
  multimedia, visit:


  http://chandra.nasa.gov

How Life Appeared On Earth


                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Nov > Nov 16

                                                     How Life Appeared On Earth
  From: Diana Cammack <cammack.nul>
  Date: Tue, 16 Nov 2010 18:48:13 +0200
  Archived: Tue, 16 Nov 2010 16:01:05 -0500
  Subject: How Life Appeared On Earth

  Source: Discovery News
  http://tinyurl.com/256g7k5
  15-11-2010

  Life on Earth Spawned by Dead Alien Microbes?
  Ian O'Neill
  How life appeared on Earth is one of the biggest questions
  hanging over modern science. For many, the idea that simple
  lifeforms appeared from a primordial soup of amino acids is,
  well, too simple to explain the genetic diversity we see today.
  If life didn't form here in puddles of ooze, where did it come
  from?
  One exciting hypothesis is that life came from an interstellar
  origin; hitched a ride on a speck of dust, blew out of its host
  star system by stellar winds and then ended up on our planet.
  This concept is called "panspermia."
  WATCH VIDEO: Will the real ET be little green men or little
  green bacteria?
  SLIDE SHOW: Top 10 Places to Find Alien Life
  Alas, as highlighted by a research paper published in the
  journal Space Science Review, assuming that life - no matter how
  hardy - could dodge death from space radiation, vacuum and
  extreme cold, is a stretch to say the least.
  "Biologically, the destructive effects of ultraviolet light and
  cosmic rays means that the majority of organisms arrive broken
  and dead on a new world," Paul Wesson, a visiting researcher at
  the Herzberg Institute of Astrophysics in Canada, says in the
  abstract of Panspermia, Past and Present: Astrophysical and
  Biophysical Conditions for the Dissemination of Life in Space.
  "The likelihood of conventional forms of panspermia must
  therefore be considered low."
  Although these comments don't necessarily rule out panspermia as
  a cosmic life-spreading mechanism, Wesson does raise an
  important point. Although we know terrestrial life can survive
  for long periods in space - whether it's cyanobacteria living
  outside the space station for 553 days or tortured mosquito
  larvae - we've only tested how life copes in space for months or
  years. Panspermia is thought to operate over much longer
  timescales, possibly millions of years.
  Sure, one could envisage a short hop from planet-to-planet
  within our own solar system - remember the stir of Mars
  meteorite ALH84001 (pictured top) - but for longer, interstellar
  journeys, panspermia via stellar wind-blown dust isn't
  realistic.
  ANALYSIS: Directed Panspermia: Moral Obligation or Bio-
  Pollution?


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/nov/m16-010.shtml[12/12/2011 09:41:02]
How Life Appeared On Earth

  This is why Wesson suggests the panspermia concept may need to
  be modified for interstellar hops when life dies and its genetic
  chemical bonds degrade in transit. Although it sounds more like
  the title of a sci-fi horror movie, Wesson has coined the term
  "necropanspermia" to describe a rather macabre means of
  transferring life around the cosmos. Dead organisms could spawn
  new life.
  As noted by Physorg.com writer Lin Edwards, Wesson doesn't
  actually suggest a mechanism by which these long-dead alien
  interlopers spawn new life, only that his research suggests
  life's "information" is transferred to new worlds. If the
  destination is hospitable, the conditions might be right for the
  dead microbes' genetic code to be reassembled.
  "Resurrection may, however, be possible." Wesson concludes.
  "Certain micro-organisms possess remarkably effective enzyme
  systems that can repair a multitude of strand breaks."
  Alas, necropanspermia - like all incarnations of the panspermia
  concept - will remain pure conjecture for now. But Wesson
  suggests that to test his hypothesis, dust grains from the
  outermost regions of the solar system (to avoid contamination by
  Earth Brand=99 life) could be collected to see if they contain
  information that may indicate they contain long-dead
  interstellar microbial life.
  I personally find this research fascinating, but it's a shame
  necropanspermia wasn't conceived before the 1997 sci-fi horror
  flick Alien: Resurrection was written; reanimated aliens -
  rather than terrible alien-human hybrids - as the central plot
  line would have been awesome.
  Publication: Panspermia, Past and Present: Astrophysical and
  Biophysical Conditions for the Dissemination of Life in Space,
  Paul S. Wesson, Space Science Reviews, 2010. DOI:
  10.1007/s11214-010-9671-x

  Source: Physorg.com

                                                   Re: Crop Circle Maker Tells All
  From: J. Maynard Gelinas <j.maynard.gelinas.nul>
  Date: Tue, 16 Nov 2010 17:11:33 -0500
  Archived: Wed, 17 Nov 2010 07:25:51 -0500
  Subject: Re: Crop Circle Maker Tells All

  >From: Kathy Kasten <catraja.nul>
  >To: <post.nul>
  >Date: Mon, 15 Nov 2010 18:26:27 +0000
  >Subject: Re: Crop Circle Maker Tells All
  <snip>
  >If you wish to solve the paradox problem and issue of creating a
  >crop circle induces a so-called paranormal response, I suggest
  >reading material covering entanglement theory. It suggests that
  >focusing energy of the human mind creates a physical response.
  >And, yes, isn't that what used to be called magic?
  I'm not a physicist. Unlike Sereda, I don't even play one on TV!
  But I have read a few books and watched these wonderful video
  lectures by the late Cal Tech physics Professor and Nobel Prize
  winner Richard Feynman. If the professional physicists like him
  are to be believed, there's no magic involved. At the link are
  about five hours of wonderful lectures by Feynman. These
  lectures are an excellent resource for laypersons wanting a good
  basic understanding of quantum mechanics without cracking open a
  textbook on the subject. He had a real gift for explaining the
  esoteric and abstruse in layman's language.
  http://vega.org.uk/video/subseries/8
  But the idea is pretty simple. Assuming you had the energy
  available to generate EM waves at a small enough frequency to
  resolve a subatomic particle, like a proton or neutron (it would
  be a silly-large amount of energy), by striking that subatomic
  particle with this super high energy photon you would - by
  definition - impart enough kinetic energy to divert its path
  through space. That is, since bouncing EM waves off of stuff and
  then capturing their changed vector with a photodetector is the
  only method of detection currently known, and since a photon
  imparts energy on whatever is hit, it is impossible to
  simultaneously detect both the momentum and direction of a
  moving subatomic particle. Because the detection method - EM
  waves - changes the state of the particle after detection. It's
  that simple.
  Think of it like a moving ball on a billiards table in a dark
  room. One can't see the moving ball, but one could roll another
  ball into its path and then record the soundwave of them
  striking one another. From that one could determine the location
  of the ball on the table at the _instant_ it had been hit. But
  after that point, one would still be just as clueless as to the
  next location of the target ball, for after having been hit its
  vector and speed would have changed unpredictably.
  No magic!
  -M

  From: Gerald O'Connell <goc.nul>

  Subject: Re: How Life Appeared On Earth

  >From: Diana Cammack <cammack.nul>
  >To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >Date: Tue, 16 Nov 2010 18:48:13 +0200
  >Subject: How Life Appeared On Earth
  >Source: Discovery News
  >http://tinyurl.com/256g7k5
  >15-11-2010
  >Life on Earth Spawned by Dead Alien Microbes?
  >Ian O'Neill
  >How life appeared on Earth is one of the biggest questions
  >hanging over modern science. For many, the idea that simple
  >lifeforms appeared from a primordial soup of amino acids is,
  >well, too simple to explain the genetic diversity we see today.
  >If life didn't form here in puddles of ooze, where did it come
  >from?
  >One exciting hypothesis is that life came from an interstellar
  >origin; hitched a ride on a speck of dust, blew out of its host
  >star system by stellar winds and then ended up on our planet.
  >This concept is called "panspermia."
  Great idea.
  And how did that life form in the first place? Somewhere with
  better, richer, puddles of ooze?
  What's wrong with our primordial soup? Which elements or
  compounds would be missing?
  In any case, once life gets under way, that means self-
  replicating complexity has taken over, creating new compounds
  and adapting to exploit those already available. It develops
  according to its own rules, not according to a mere list of
  ingredients on the primordial soup can. Life creates its own
  recipe.
  --
  Gerald O'Connell
  http://www.onlyport.com

                                                      British Invisibility Research
   From: Dave Haith <visions1.nul>
   Date: Wed, 17 Nov 2010 13:44:49 -0000
   Archived: Thu, 18 Nov 2010 09:13:45 -0500
   Subject: British Invisibility Research

   UFOs sometimes disappear instantly.
   Some presume this to be because they move off at an incredible
   speed.
   Others consider whether there is a technology being used to
   create invisibility.
   In this video a Scots scientist reckons such technology may be
   only a couple of years away using some form of light refraction:
   http://vimeo.com/6177122
   In the following linked article he dips much more deeply into
   the world of potential invisibility by detailing research using
   fibre optics to cloak events by shifting them into a different
   time channel!
   Space=96Time Cloak Could Hide Events
   http://www.nature.com/news/2010/101116/full/news.2010.611.html
   Frankly it's beyond me but the techno boffins on this List might
   be interested.

   Dave Haith

  is derived from information from multiple sources including this
  list. I filter the information by considering the source and
  other criteria, and then take it as evidence for or against the
  validity of certain points of view.
  Everything we now think we know is subject to change, and to a
  greater or lesser degree, much of what we experience is "simply
  an inference, circumstantial, subject to misinterpretation or
  misidentification, and thus only anecdotal and speculative. It
  may or may not be as perceived or interpreted." So I agree with
  that.
  When Don says that the US does not fly secret triangular craft,
  that is his opinion based on what he thinks is true. I merely
  pointed out that my interpretation of events is consistent with
  the opposite point of view. For example, take the Stephenville
  incident where it was reported that an anomalous craft slowly
  approached the President's ranch. There were fighter planes in
  the air, but no apparent challenge to its approach. There are
  several ways to interpret that. The only one consistent with
  alien invader is that the fighters were totally outclassed and
  they knew it. The more likely alternative is that the incursion
  was acceptable to the military because it was one of their own.
  Don would disagree because it is unthinkable to him that such
  craft would be test flown over populated areas. That seems
  right, and would mean that it most likely was not a test flight
  but a fully operational craft.

  William

  As it turned out, the craft could go where they wanted without
  permission from the Belgian Air Force. These craft would not
  necessarily have been American, and it's possible they were not
  controlled by any one nation. There may exist a power structure
  that most of us know nothing about. Richard Dolan's idea of the
  existence of a breakaway civilization made possible by the new
  technology is mind-blowing but not impossible.
  >The argument, suggested by some, of a secret "socio -
  >psychological" experiment does not make sense either (if not
  >alien). Just imagine the enormous risk of an engine failure and
  >crash on the main square of a Belgian village.
  I live under a flight path to an airport, and I see many
  aircraft fly over my house. No one seems too concerned that I
  might be in any danger. So the risk of a crash onto a Belgian
  village could well have been minimal - even non-existent, since
  we don't know the capabilities or reliability of the craft in
  question.
  In any case, I have never taken the position that all UFOs are
  human-made. I have decided that in today's world, where there is
  so much jaw-dropping deception by officialdom in relation to
  geopolitics, that I should remain open to the possibility that
  some are of terrestrial origin. Perhaps the capability is fairly
  recent, possibly beginning just before the wave in Belgium.
  According to Dolan's book, the first flap of triangle UFOs
  occurred in 1975 in North Carolina.
  >So, the mystery remains, it seems to me. And some peole may
  >still be trying to hide it.

The Real X Files Are In Uruguay


                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Nov > Nov 18

                                                  The Real X Files Are In Uruguay
  From: A. J. Gevaerd <aj.nul>
  Date: Wed, 17 Nov 2010 15:37:58 -0200
  Archived: Thu, 18 Nov 2010 09:54:50 -0500
  Subject: The Real X Files Are In Uruguay

  Dear Listers:
  I just arrived home from Uruguay where I gave a lecture about
  the progress of the Brazilian UFO disclosure and I interviewed
  the colonel Ariel Sanchez, navigator and flight controller of
  the Uruguayan Air Force (FAU) who is also the commander of the
  Commission for Reception and Inquiry of Unidentified Flying
  Objects Reports (CRIDOVNI), one of the few OFFICIAL UFO groups
  on planet Earth whose existence is known openly, along with
  Chilean CEFAA and Peruvian OIFAA.
  There is no secret at all about it in Uruguay and the population
  knows about it and how the office works, which is official
  because it was created by a government decision in 1979.
  In the country of about 3 million inhabitants, CRIDOVNI does a
  kind of Ufology that is unique and does not exist anywhere else
  in the world. It is a OFFICIAL UFOLOGY. Members of CRIDOVNI are
  in fact professionals ufologists, with their salaries been paid
  by the FAU. They serve the air force in regular activities and
  use part of their time to do official UFO researches, standing
  by for calls and field investigations.
  CRIDOVNI has two offices, one in downtown Montevideo and other
  inside the headquarters of FAU at Borto Lanza Airfield, and it
  counts with the assistance of laboratories, universities and
  several institutes spread around the country to help in their
  researches. All these institutes feel proud to collaborate with
  CRIDOVNI.
  I spent Sunday, November 14th, with Daniel Silvera, Secretary of
  the organization, who told me the details of the foundation and
  CRIDOVNI's operations. It was an extremely interesting
  conversation. Silvera said that, like others, he can receive
  calls anytime from anywhere in the country from someone
  reporting a UFO. Then, they follow a routine that is part of
  their lives for years: the coordination of CRIDOVNI meets
  immediately to decide who will follow the call, and there goes
  the team convened to the field, normally dressed as air force
  military, in official cars and with cameras equipment etc. Very
  professional.
  They arrived on site, talk to witnesses, raise all available
  data, collect samples and evidences (if any), and return to the
  capital to begin the investigation, following a rigorous
  methodological standard.
  CRIDOVNI personnel is meticulous and detailled people, and all
  have technical skills. Some are engineers, other chemists, some
  professors, a few flight controllers, some psychologists etc.
  They apply to the exhaust the scientific research method, which
  has also been exported to other countries, like Peru, where
  theAnomalous Aerial Phenomena Investigation Office (OIFAA)
  applies them. (By the way, I am also interviewing the commander
  of the OIFAA, colonel Julio Chamorro, with sensational
  revelations.)
  I want to say that guys like Daniel and Ariel, there in Uruguay,
  among many other members of CRIDOVNI, always on stand by to any


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/nov/m18-006.shtml[12/12/2011 09:41:10]
The Real X Files Are In Uruguay

  event involving UFOs, are the real Fox Mulder and Danna Scully,
  and the UFO cases that they investigate are the legitimate X-
  Files.
  It's been a great thrill to know how CRIDOVNI's structure works.
  And to wonder that Brazil did it at the same level in 1969 with
  the System f orInvestigation of Unidentified Aerial Objects
  (SIOANI) in S=C3=A3o Paulo! Yes! The SIOANI was like the CRIDOVNI,
  but came before - 10 years before - and was much bigger,
  evidently due to the size of our continental country.
  See photos here:
  http://tinyurl.com/2f38cvm
  Look for photo gallery.

  Best,
  A. J. Gevaerd

  >Second, a strong residual magnetic field may have persisted
  >after the parting of the object in question. Therefore, the
  >field is tied to the Earth's soil and no other fancy explanation
  >is necessary until further testing is implemented there and in
  >the surrounding area to determine, if for no other reason,
  >whether similar fields preexist or are otherwise unique to the
  >area:

  >fields with a single axis instrument.
  The complete story of the remnant magnetic field is told at:
  http://www.brumac.8k.com/MagneticUFO/MagneticUFO.html
  As pointed out there, the strongest gradient occurred when the
  gradiometer was held upward at arm's length and "pointed" at the
  tops of some 12-15 ft pine trees. Of particular iportance is
  that there was much lower response or no response when the wand
  was near the ground. Two days after the field was discovered a
  repeat search of the are found to field, even where the signal
  was strongest.
  As far as using the instrument correctly or being an
  instrumental artifact is concerned, the gradiometer operator
  checked his instrument immediately after getting the first
  unexpected response near a pond.
  An area survey of the standard grid type was not carried out,
  unfortunately. Therefore it is impossible to construct an
  complete map of the field gradient strength or the "shape" of
  the field.
  But the main aspect of this case, the presence of a field where
  there should have been none, is still a mystery.

Scientists Say ET Technology Beyond Comprehension


                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Nov > Nov 18

                                  Scientists Say ET Technology Beyond Comprehension
  From: Dave Haith <visions1.nul>
  Date: Wed, 17 Nov 2010 22:32:50 -0000
  Archived: Thu, 18 Nov 2010 10:20:13 -0500
  Subject: Scientists Say ET Technology Beyond Comprehension

  This article might interest Listers.

  -----
  Source: The Daily Galaxy
  http://tinyurl.com/3yghlob
  November 17, 2010

  "Beyond Human Comprehension" - World's Leading Scientists Riff
  On ET Technology
  Stephen Hawking warned recently that contact with an advanced
  extraterrestrial civilization could have dire consequences for
  the human species.
  Arthur C Clarke once made the famous observation that any
  sufficiently advanced technology would be indistinguishable from
  magic.
  Following in their footsteps, world renowned experts from
  physicist Sir Martin Rees of Cambridge University to
  astrobiologist Paul Davies of Arizona State have asked if we
  were to encounter alien technology far superior to our own,
  would we even realize what it was. A technology a million or
  more years in advance of ours could appear miraculous.
  In fact, Davies writes in his new book, Eerie Silence, that
  advanced technology might not even be made of matter; that it
  might have no fixed size or shape; have no well-defined
  boundaries; is dynamical on all scales of space and time.
  Or, conversely, does not appear to do anything at all that we
  can discern. Does not consist of discrete, separate things; but
  rather it is a system, or a subtle higher-level correlation of
  things.
  Are matter and information, Davies asks, all there is? Five
  hundred years ago, he writes, " the very concept of a device
  manipulating information, or software, would have been
  incomprehensible. Might there be a still higher level, as yet
  outside all human experience, that organizes electrons?
  If so, this "third level" would never be manifest through
  observations made at the informational level, still less at the
  matter level.
  We should be open to the distinct possibility that advanced
  alien technology a billion years old may operate at the third,
  or perhaps even a fourth or fifth level
   -all of which are totally incomprehensible to the human
  mind at our current state of evolution in 2010.

  Casey Kazan

  Union) all tested and flew secret triangular shaped-delta winged
  over the last 60 years. But what I was getting at was that they
  all flew conventionally, were conventionally noisy, could not
  hover soundlessly, accelerate from a standing stop to over
  thousands of miles per hour - noiselessly or otherwise.

  rather than what is aerodynamically possible with today's

  President Bush's ranch. Why is it that the the presumed security
  forces of the US take leave of their senses periodically and fly
  monsterous Top Secret craft over populated areas. There is no
  good reason for this other than to supply uninformed debunkers,
  skeptics and researchers with an explanatory argument that is
  unsupportable.

  Don Ledger

  From: Rich Heiden <rwheidenu.nul>
  Date: Wed, 17 Nov 2010 17:24:17 -0800 (PST)
  Archived: Sat, 20 Nov 2010 10:12:42 -0500
  Subject: UFO Postmark In Belleville Wisconsin

  Hi all,
  Sorry, I neglected to send this before. But you might be
  interested in the postmark for Belleville (Wis.) UFO Day (which
  was Oct. 30). It was reported in these newspapers from my state-
  wide clipping service:
  - New Glarus (Wis.) Post Messenger Recorder, Oct. 21 and 28, 2010
  (this is Belleville's local paper now, after the Belleville
  Recorder merged with another paper).
  - Evansville (Wis.) Review, Oct. 27, 2010.
  Commemorative cancellations are available for a certain period
  of time _after_ the cancellation date. A woman at the USPS's
  toll-free number says two months, but the two later articles
  above say the order needs to be postmarked by Nov. 30. Also, it
  should be possible to get unaddressed envelopes with the
  cancellation. Instead of sending the P.O. only stamped addressed
  envelopes, send stamped blank envelopes, along with a larger
  envelope for the other ones to be put inside of to be returned
  to you. Actually, you don't need envelopes=E2=80=94the cheaper postal
  cards would work just fine, and maybe you could send the
  Belleville P.O. a check for some of them (or for the pre-stamped
  postal envelopes), without having to buy them yourself and mail
  them. (Of course, the envelope you send for the return of the
  cards should have adequate postage.)
  I am planning to get several myself - I have some of the UFO-ish
  "science fiction" booklet stamps from a few years ago that I
  will use as part of the postage. If you want to do something
  like that, a local philatelic store should be able to
  accommodate you.
  Here is the address:
  U.S. Post Office
  102 S. Vine St
  Belleville, WI 53508-9183
  The USPS's Web site is SUPPOSED to have news on commemorative
  cancellations, but I can't find any. But for a picture of
  Belleville's, see:
  http://tinyurl.com/2daucrv

  Rich Heiden

  Dear List:
  Last night as I was scanning the night time sky smoking a really
  good cigar, I started thinking what if... what if Earthlings
  were really the aliens. What I mean by that is there could
  probably be an entire federation of planets located toward the
  center of the Milky Way galaxy with a completely different
  understanding of how the universe works - THE Law of Physics.
  We Earthlings, having never left our own solar system have A Law
  of Physics which is based only on how our solar system works.
  What should be clueing us into the fact that those Laws might be
  different outside our solar system is there is a wrinkle in a
  law as one of our little space probes is nearing the boundary of
  our system. It could be an indication that things could be very
  different in other places in the larger Universe.
  What started this dialog with myself is remembering an old comic
  strip involving a swamp and swamp critters who made observations
  about life on this planet. The possum was named Pogo. One of the
  observations he made that stuck with me was: "We have met the
  aliens, and they is us." What if, compared to the rest of the
  Universe, we are the aliens with strange Physical Laws, physical
  build, thoughts. The odd guys out. Hah! Wouldn't that be funny.

  KK

  Interesting you should suggest this, Eleanor. Sarg's BSM-SG
  theory actually proposes that each galaxy began with a big bang.
  According to the theory, primordial matter self-organized between
  concentric spheres to the point where there was an explosion
  resulting in both matter as we know it and the galactic aether, a
  dynamic lattice structure with properties that account for a
  number of common physical constants and phenomena. The galactic
  center presently consists of the remnants of this explosion. He
  says the theory predicts the gamma ray bubbles recently observed
  near the galactic center.
  The following is his explanation of the bubbles.
  -----
  While this new discovery confuses the astrophysicists it is in full
  agreement with the BSM-SG concept about the birth of any individual
  galaxy. (There is no Big Bang, Universe is static. Every galaxy has
  own cycle of active existence, collapse, hidden recycling phase and
  birth of a new galaxy. Some stars that escaped the collapse are
  separate islands and in the new born galaxy they form Globular
  Clusters containing thousands closed spaced stars with strange
  behavior).
  The Giant gamma ray bubble according to BSM-SG theory is a
  signature of the supermassive primordial matter that exists in
  the Milky way center (and in every galaxy). According to the
  scenario of the galaxy birth presented in Chapter 12 of BSM-SG
  the whole matter before the birth of the galaxy is enclosed in a
  Galactic Egg that has two shells: external and internal.
  The internal shell enclosed a superdense primordial bulk matter.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/nov/m20-005.shtml[12/12/2011 09:41:19]
Re: Galaxies Older Than The Big Bang?

  The elementary particles for the whole unborn new galaxy
  crystallize between the two egg shells. The whole Galactic Egg
  rotates in respect to the Universe frame of reference. At some
  moment when the crystallization process is complete, the
  external egg shells breaks and the galaxy explodes -- a new
  galaxy is born.
  The signature of this is a huge gamma ray burst -- the most
  powerful detectable explosion that confuses the astrophysicists.
  The internal egg shell is left and continues to rotate. The
  crystallized matter (stable elementary particles) is spread in a
  plane perpendicular to rotational axis and forms the galaxy with
  a shape of a pancake. The rotating internal shell is only from
  one type of prisms that obtains a huge electrical charge -- maybe
  the positive. This charge attracts the electrons and they form
  clouds. In these clouds the electrons begin to oscillate with
  the positrons forming electron-positron pairs. They emit X and
  gamma rays. Externally the electron-positron pairs are neutral
  so they are not attracted by the superstrong charge of the
  galactic nucleus but only by its gravity forces. For this reason
  they will form clouds. The centrifugal forces are smaller along
  the axis of rotation of the supermassive galactic nucleus, so
  the oscillating electron-positron pair will form bubbles aligned
  along this axis
  -----

  Good friend and UFO researcher George Wingfield, now resident in
  the US, has just written to me these comments:
  -----
  "No, Dave, that's not a skyfish, UFO or a streak of New Age
  earth energy over the Tor! The answer is far more mundane. I'm
  99% sure that it's a bird flying swiftly across the field of the
  camera probably no more than about 10 - 15 feet away. If you
  look closely you can see what appear to be faint images of
  raised wings at the beginning (left) and end (right) of its
  flight path. I'd guess it was a pigeon.
  There is no reason for thinking this streak is behind the top of
  the Tor at a considerable distance from the camera. If it is
  about ten feet away, flying at, say, 60 mph (88 feet per sec) it
  would move about 9 feet in a tenth of a second. I suggest that
  on a dark day - which this appears to be - the automatically set
  exposure time might be about that.

  In all my years of living near Glastonbury I've walked on the
  Tor many hundreds of times but never seen UFOs there. Not that
  I haven't seen UFOs elsewhere. However it may encourage you to
  know that John Michell writing about his visits to the Tor in
  the late 1960s wrote :-

  .....we used to watch the nightly manoeuverings of lights in the
  sky, even on one occasion seeing a luminous "mother ship" with
  smaller lights going in and out of it.

  Then John and his hippy friends were, at the time, smoking
  certain substances which I didn't have with me when I was
  walking my dogs on the Tor!

  George Wingfield

  P.S. You are right: so-called "Rods" which many photographers

  From: Ron Eastwood <rge.nul>
  Date: Sat, 20 Nov 2010 14:42:35 -0800

  Subject: Re: Glastonbury Tor Photo Anomaly

  >From: Dave Haith visions1.nul

  >Date: Sat, 20 Nov 2010

  >http://www.glastonburydogwalkers.com/blog

  >three photos taken on Glastonbury Tor in Somerset, England.
  >Unsure of taking the Sun newspaper's UFO expert as a purveyor
  >of all truth! - I would rather ask this List's resident photo
  >analysts what they make of this - which reminds me a little of
  >the famous 'rods' or 'skyfish' which I thought have now been
  >discredited.

  Dave Haith, et.al.,
  I have begun to take the philosophical position on all the
  anomolous photos and personal accounts of high strangeness (as
  Lind Moulton Howe has coined it) which are flooding the internet
  and now even some of the main line news agencies of the world...
  the position that each event is just what it appears to be or
  just what the writer claims it to be until further evidence to
  the contrary surfaces. That is, unless it comes from Dr. Richard
  Boylan, in which case it is classified as "BS" immediately and
  will only be resurected as "possible" if and when even one
  single bit of verifiable collaborative evidence surfaces!
  Having said that about that I proceed with my developing
  criteria at establishing the "reality" or even "plausible
  reality" of anomalous subjects. If the photo or description
  comes from a reasonably well established investigator or
  authority I tend to give it much greater credance than from a
  first time experiencer or unknown individual. Either way I then
  wait to see if the person shoots down their own discovery by
  further additional information. For example if someone comes up
  with photos of an extra terrestrial UFO then claims to be an
  abductee then starts to make a lucrative income from sale of
  books, CDs, lectures, etc. on his/her proclaimed discovery or
  experience or new found ability to channel communications which
  no one else is receiving... then I strongly tend to write off
  everything they offer until subsequent verification begins to
  show up.
  I am willing to take the risk of treating the claims of
  experiencers and observers as valid until something proves them
  a hoax. If something looks like a whispy translucent vibrating
  mile long jelly fish like entity swimming through the air then I
  will keep an open mind as accept that it is what it appeared. Of
  course I try to find reasonable explanations to account for the
  anomaly (glare on the camera lense, tiny insect close to lense,
  digital processing artifact, etc.) But if none are found to be
  replicatable under the same conditions, then I will for the time
  being accept that we have a new "reality" amongst us. No harm
  (yet).
  I would rather take that open minded approach than just out


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/nov/m21-001.shtml[12/12/2011 09:41:21]
Re: Glastonbury Tor Photo Anomaly

  right deny the possibility of strangeness just because it makes
  us uncombortable or leaves us scratching our head in
  uncertainty. I like to say to my friends when this sort of issue
  arises it is like the first time I walked barefoot on a three or
  four inch thick bed of glowing embers from an all day fire which
  reduced several thousand oak fire logs into a quarter mile long,
  20 ft. wide, path of fire. We watched as the leaders took the
  first steps unharmed and then most of us followed. To my
  disbelief it not only did not injure my feet but we were able to
  bend over and pick up hand fulls of golf ball sized glowing
  embers and hold in our bare hands with no ill effect. No magic.
  No special grease on our skin. I still do not understand nor
  know how, but I definitely "believe" fire walking is possible.
  Having now done it several more times it is still just as
  amazing. So, I hold out for accepting the preposterous, allowing
  for the seemingly impossible or implausible, and just letting
  time tell wether or not it is a trick or a hoax.
  There Dave. Dose that clarify the waters for you? Grin.

  Peace, Ron

  guess at. Try this one on for size. Werner von Braun was said to
  have expected a false flag operation that would be blamed on
  real ETs. An enemy in space would justify the expenditure of
  many more tax dollars on black project R&D. But first there
  would have to be a perception that ET is real and already
  visiting our planet. Hence, we see the blatant exposure of
  certain advanced technology craft to the general population.

  would never believe that it is secret human technology. Take
  yourself, Don, as an example.

  at some point towards human targets by the black triangle craft.
  This would happen only after there is general agreement in the
  population that the craft are operated by ETs.

  rationale why they are taking the risk of crashing in a
  populated area.
